Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Parallelism:** T1 (clone/catalog evals), T3 (model validation), and T0 (hermes-megaplan prerequisite) can all start immediately in parallel. T2 depends on T1. T4/T5/T6 depend on T2 (schema). T7-T9 are the core orchestrator and depend on T0 (hard blocker) + T4. T10 parallels T7-T9. T12 needs all implementation done. T13-T14 are sequential validation.\n\n**Biggest risk \u2014 T2 gate:** If EVAL.ts files overwhelmingly depend on Vercel-internal observability (Tier 3 fields), the scoring system becomes build-only and the eval results are much less meaningful. This is the earliest go/no-go decision point. Do T2 thoroughly before investing in T6 scoring implementation.\n\n**Four unresolved flags the gate passed through \u2014 executor must fix:**\n\n1. **scoring-git-diff-base-invalid:** The plan says `git diff HEAD~1` but there's only one commit in the workspace. FIX: capture initial_commit_sha from `git rev-parse HEAD` after the initial commit in prepare_workspace(), then diff against that SHA after execution. Or diff against the empty tree hash (`git diff 4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf899d15d4a9a96 HEAD`).\n\n2. **trace-capture-session-log-behavior-mismatched:** Plan says ~/.hermes/logs/ but actual path is ~/.hermes/sessions/. > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

Do T2 thoroughly before investing in T6 scoring implementation.\n\n**Four unresolved flags the gate passed through \u2014 executor must fix:**\n\n1. **scoring-git-diff-base-invalid:** The plan says `git diff HEAD~1` but there's only one commit in the workspace. Also, sessions are REUSED per agent role across iterations (plan+revise share one session ID, all critiques share one, all gates share one). Any code referencing output, input_tokens, or output_tokens is wrong. {
  "cmd": "python - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport json\nfrom collections import Counter,defaultdict\npaths = sorted(Path([REDACTED]).glob('**/run-*/result.json'))\nroot_keys = Counter()\no11y_keys = Counter()\nstatus_vals = Counter()\ntool_keys = Counter()\nscript_keys = Counter()\nfile_types = defaultdict(set)\nfor p in paths:\n    data = json.loads(p.read_text())\n    for k,v in data.items():\n        root_keys[k]+=1\n        file_types[f'root.{k}'].add(type(v).__name__)\n    o11y = data.get('o11y', {})\n    if isinstance(o11y, dict):\n        for k,v in o11y.items():\n            o11y_keys[k]+=1\n            file_types[f'o11y.{k}'].add(type(v).__name__)\n        tc = o11y.get('toolCalls', {})\n        if isinstance(tc, dict):\n            for k,v in tc.items():\n                tool_keys[k]+=1\n                file_types[f'toolCalls.{k}'].add(type(v).__name__)\n    op = data.get('outputPaths', {})\n    if isinstance(op, dict):\n        scripts = op.get('scripts', {})\n        if isinstance(scripts, dict):\n            for k,v in scripts.items():\n                script_keys[k]+=1\n                file_types[f'outputPaths.scripts.{k}'].add(type(v).__name__)\n    status_vals[data.get('status')] += 1\nprint('count', len(paths))\nprint('root_keys', root_keys)\nprint('o11y_keys', o11y_keys)\nprint('status_vals', status_vals)\nprint('tool_keys', tool_keys)\nprint('script_keys', script_keys)\nfor k in sorted(file_types):\n    print(k, sorted(file_types[k]))\nPY",
  "workdir": "/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent",
  "yield_time_ms": 1000,
  "max_output_tokens": 16000
}

from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/next-evals-oss/evals`\n+- 889 historical `result.json` files from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/next-evals-oss/results`\n+\n+## Gate Verdict\n+\n+Gate result: related observability fields.\n+- Tier 3 dependency rate across the current eval assertions is therefore `0%`.\n+- The current eval suite is overwhelmingly source-tree and file-content based, so scoring does not need a Vercel-only observability dependency to stay viable.\n+\n+Implication:\n+- The scoring approach can proceed.\n+- `result.json` still needs to be generated for compatibility with the existing `@vercel/agent-eval` result shape and for future-proofing, but it is not on the critical path for the current hidden assertions.\n+\n+## Tier Classification\n+\n+### Tier 1: Git diff derivable\n+\n+These can be computed directly from the prepared workspace and the captured initial commit SHA.\n+\n+| Field | Notes |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `o11y.filesModified` | Best derived from `git diff --name-only <initial_commit_sha>` after execution. |\n+\n+### Tier 2: Hermes trace or orchestrator derivable\n+\n+These can be built from Hermes session logs, tool traces, shell execution records, and orchestrator metadata.\n+\n+| Field | Source |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `status` | Orchestrator verdict (`passed` / `failed`) |\n+| `duration` | Wall-clock runtime |\n+| `model` | Configured experiment model label |\n+| `error` | Orchestrator/runtime failure string |\n+| `transcriptPath` | Audit artifact path |\n+| `transcriptRawPath` | Audit artifact path |\n+| `outputPaths.eval` | Eval output artifact path |\n+| `outputPaths.scripts.build` | Build output artifact path |\n+| `o11y.errors` | Trace/tool/runtime errors |\n+| `o11y.filesRead` | File-read tool invocations or trace-derived file access |\n+| `o11y.shellCommands` | Terminal tool invocations |\n+| `o11y.thinkingBlocks` | Assistant reasoning block count from normalized messages |\n+| `o11y.toolCalls.*` | Tool invocation counts after mapping Hermes tool names to framework buckets |\n+| `o11y.totalToolCalls` | Sum of mapped tool calls |\n+| `o11y.totalTurns` | Message/turn count from normalized transcript |\n+| `o11y.webFetches` | Web tool traces if present |\n+\n+### Tier 3: Vercel framework only / stub candidates\n+\n+Current finding:\n+- No current `EVAL.ts` assertions depend on Tier 3-only fields.\n+- No extra Tier 3-only keys were required to describe the sampled `result.json` corpus.\n+\n+Practical interpretation:\n+- For this snapshot, Tier 3 is effectively empty.\n+- If future evals begin asserting framework-specific observability that Hermes cannot reconstruct, those new fields should be stubbed explicitly and the gate should be revisited.\n+\n+## Sampled `result.json` Shape\n+\n+Observed root keys across 889 sampled results:\n+- Always present: `status`, `duration`, `model`\n+- Present on successful/fully-observed runs: `o11y`, `outputPaths`, `transcriptPath`, `transcriptRawPath`\n+- Present on infra-style failures: `error`\n+\n+Observed `o11y.toolCalls` buckets:\n+- `agent_task`\n+- `file_edit`\n+- `file_read`\n+- `file_write`\n+- `glob`\n+- `grep`\n+- `list_dir`\n+- `shell`\n+- `unknown`\n+- `web_fetch`\n+- `web_search`\n+\n+Observed `shellCommands` item shape:\n+- Required: `command`\n+- Optional: `success`, `exitCode`\n+\n+## Mismatch With Plan Assumption\n+\n+The batch plan assumed current `EVAL.ts` files consume `__agent_eval__/results.json`.\n+\n+Current snapshot finding:\n+- They do not.\n+- The real compatibility target is the framework-shaped `result.json` corpus already stored under `next-evals-oss/results/`.\n+\n+This is favorable for the Hermes integration: the scoring gate passes, and `result.json` generation can stay lightweight and trace-backed rather than Vercel-runtime dependent.\n*** End Patch\n"

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Parallelism:** T1 (clone/catalog evals), T3 (model validation), and T0 (hermes-megaplan prerequisite) can all start immediately in parallel. T2 depends on T1. T4/T5/T6 depend on T2 (schema). T7-T9 are the core orchestrator and depend on T0 (hard blocker) + T4. T10 parallels T7-T9. T12 needs all implementation done. T13-T14 are sequential validation.\n\n**Biggest risk \u2014 T2 gate:** If EVAL.ts files overwhelmingly depend on Vercel-internal observability (Tier 3 fields), the scoring system becomes build-only and the eval results are much less meaningful. This is the earliest go/no-go decision point. Do T2 thoroughly before investing in T6 scoring implementation.\n\n**Four unresolved flags the gate passed through \u2014 executor must fix:**\n\n1. **scoring-git-diff-base-invalid:** The plan says `git diff HEAD~1` but there's only one commit in the workspace. FIX: capture initial_commit_sha from `git rev-parse HEAD` after the initial commit in prepare_workspace(), then diff against that SHA after execution. Or diff against the empty tree hash (`git diff 4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf899d15d4a9a96 HEAD`).\n\n2. **trace-capture-session-log-behavior-mismatched:** Plan says ~/.hermes/logs/ but actual path is ~/.hermes/sessions/. Also, sessions are REUSED per agent role across iterations (plan+revise share one session ID, all critiques share one, all gates share one). FIX: Before each megaplan CLI call, read the session log, record current message count as offset. After the call, re-read the log and slice messages[offset:] to get only that phase's messages. The session_id comes from state.json sessions object keyed by role name.\n\n3. **auditability-raw-api-capture-not-planned:** Accepted scope boundary (DECISION-004). Document in config schema and audit README that raw HTTP payloads are NOT captured \u2014 only normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(). This is sufficient for the experiment goals.\n\n4. **config-openrouter-params-not-plumbed:** Accepted scope boundary. openrouter_params in config are for documentation/reproducibility only. Add a clear comment in config.py and config JSON files that these values are NOT applied at runtime until Hermes/megaplan add inference parameter plumbing.\n\n**WorkerResult contract \u2014 get this right:** The actual fields are payload, raw_output, duration_ms, cost_usd, session_id, trace_output. Any code referencing output, input_tokens, or output_tokens is wrong. The worker function signature must be (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...) matching existing claude/codex workers.\n\n**Settled decisions (do not revisit):**\n- DECISION-001: Shell out to megaplan CLI, don't import as library\n- DECISION-002: File modification via git diff against initial commit, not tool-call mapping\n- DECISION-003: Traces from session log files, not SessionDB.get_messages_as_conversation()\n- DECISION-004: Cost from run_conversation() aggregates, not raw HTTP headers\n- DECISION-005: Phase machine is init\u2192plan\u2192critique\u2192gate\u2192(revise\u2192critique\u2192gate)*\u2192finalize\u2192execute\u2192review\n\n**No evals/ directory exists yet** \u2014 all files are net-new creation. Structure: evals/{run_evals.py, config.py, scoring.py, audit.py, __init__.py}, evals/configs/{minimax-glm5.json, default.json}, evals/schemas/{results.schema.json}."

in messages:\n+        if message.get(\"role\") != \"tool\":\n+            continue\n+        tool_call_id = message.get(\"tool_call_id\")\n+        if not isinstance(tool_call_id, str) or not tool_call_id:\n+            continue\n+        payload = _parse_json_maybe(message.get(\"content\"))\n+        indexed[tool_call_id] = payload if isinstance(payload, dict) else {}\n+    return indexed\n+\n+\n+def _message_has_reasoning(message: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:\n+    if message.get(\"reasoning\"):\n+        return True\n+    details = message.get(\"reasoning_details\")\n+    if isinstance(details, list) and details:\n+        return True\n+    codex_items = message.get(\"codex_reasoning_items\")\n+    return isinstance(codex_items, list) and bool(codex_items)\n+\n+\n+def _extract_tool_name(tool_call: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n+    function = tool_call.get(\"function\")\n+    if isinstance(function, dict):\n+        name = function.get(\"name\")\n+        if isinstance(name, str):\n+            return name\n+    name = tool_call.get(\"name\")\n+    return name if isinstance(name, str) else \"\"\n+\n+\n+def _extract_tool_call_id(tool_call: dict[str, Any]) -> str | None:\n+    for key in (\"call_id\", \"id\"):\n+        value = tool_call.get(key)\n+        if isinstance(value, str) and value:\n+            return value\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def _parse_tool_arguments(tool_call: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n+    function = tool_call.get(\"function\")\n+    if not isinstance(function, dict):\n+        return {}\n+    arguments = function.get(\"arguments\")\n+    parsed = _parse_json_maybe(arguments)\n+    return parsed if isinstance(parsed, dict) else {}\n+\n+\n+def _parse_json_maybe(value: Any) -> Any:\n+    if isinstance(value, (dict, list)):\n+        return value\n+    if not isinstance(value, str):\n+        return value\n+    text = value.strip()\n+    if not text:\n+        return None\n+    try:\n+        return json.loads(text)\n+    except json.JSONDecodeError:\n+        return None\n+\n+\n+def _tool_bucket(tool_name: str) -> str:\n+    lowered = tool_name.lower()\n+    if lowered in {\"delegate_task\", \"agent_task\"}:\n+        return \"agent_task\"\n+    if lowered in {\"read_file\", \"read_files\", \"view_file\"}:\n+        return \"file_read\"\n+    if lowered in {\"write_file\", \"create_file\", \"append_file\"}:\n+        return \"file_write\"\n+    if lowered in {\"edit_file\", \"apply_patch\", \"replace_in_file\"}:\n+        return \"file_edit\"\n+    if lowered in {\"glob_search\", \"glob_files\", \"glob\"}:\n+        return \"glob\"\n+    if lowered in {\"search_files\", \"grep_search\", \"grep\"}:\n+        return \"grep\"\n+    if lowered in {\"list_dir\", \"list_files\", \"directory_tree\"}:\n+        return \"list_dir\"\n+    if lowered in {\"terminal\", \"shell\"}:\n+        return \"shell\"\n+    if lowered in {\"web_extract\", \"web_fetch\", \"browser_navigate\"}:\n+        return \"web_fetch\"\n+    if lowered in {\"web_search\"}:\n+        return \"web_search\"\n+    if \"read\" in lowered and \"file\" in lowered:\n+        return \"file_read\"\n+    if any(token in lowered for token in (\"write\", \"create\", \"append\")) and \"file\" in lowered:\n+        return \"file_write\"\n+    if any(token in lowered for token in (\"edit\", \"patch\", \"replace\")):\n+        return \"file_edit\"\n+    if \"grep\" in lowered or \"search\" in lowered:\n+        return \"grep\"\n+    if \"glob\" in lowered:\n+        return \"glob\"\n+    if \"list\" in lowered or \"dir\" in lowered:\n+        return \"list_dir\"\n+    return \"unknown\"\n+\n+\n+def _extract_paths_for_bucket(bucket: str, arguments: dict[str, Any]) -> set[str]:\n+    if bucket != \"file_read\":\n+        return set()\n+    return set(_gather_path_values(arguments))\n+\n+\n+def _gather_path_values(value: Any) -> list[str]:\n+    found: list[str] = []\n+    if isinstance(value, dict):\n+        for key, nested in value.items():\n+            lowered = key.lower()\n+            if lowered in {\"path\", \"paths\", \"file\", \"files\", \"filepath\", \"file_path\"}:\n+                if isinstance(nested, str):\n+                    found.append(nested)\n+                elif isinstance(nested, list):\n+                    found.extend(item for item in nested if isinstance(item, str))\n+            else:\n+                found.extend(_gather_path_values(nested))\n+    elif isinstance(value, list):\n+        for item in value:\n+            found.extend(_gather_path_values(item))\n+    return found\n+\n+\n+def _shell_command_from_args(arguments: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n+    command = arguments.get(\"command\")\n+    if isinstance(command, str):\n+        return command\n+    cmd = arguments.get(\"cmd\")\n+    return cmd if isinstance(cmd, str) else \"\"\n+\n+\n+def _extract_exit_code(result_payload: dict[str, Any]) -> int | None:\n+    if not isinstance(result_payload, dict):\n+        return None\n+    for key in (\"exit_code\", \"exitCode\", \"returncode\", \"return_code\"):\n+        value = result_payload.get(key)\n+        if isinstance(value, int):\n+            return value\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def _extract_web_fetch(arguments: dict[str, Any]) -> Any:\n+    for key in (\"url\", \"urls\", \"query\"):\n+        value = arguments.get(key)\n+        if value:\n+            return value\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def _extract_error(result_payload: Any) -> str | None:\n+    if not isinstance(result_payload, dict):\n+        return None\n+    for key in (\"error\", \"stderr\", \"message\"):\n+        value = result_payload.get(key)\n+        if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip():\n+            if key != \"message\" or result_payload.get(\"success\") is False:\n+                return value.strip()\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def _git_diff_name_only(workspace: Path, initial_commit_sha: str) -> list[str]:\n+    completed = subprocess.run(\n+        [\"git\", \"diff\", \"--name-only\", initial_commit_sha, \"HEAD\"],\n+        cwd=workspace,\n+        capture_output=True,\n+        text=True,\n+        check=False,\n+    )\n+    if completed.returncode != 0:\n+        return []\n+    return [line for line in completed.stdout.splitlines() if line.strip()]\n+\n+\n+def _extract_reporter_json(stdout: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n+    text = stdout.strip()\n+    if not text:\n+        return None\n+    try:\n+        parsed = json.loads(text)\n+    except json.JSONDecodeError:\n+        parsed = None\n+    if isinstance(parsed, dict):\n+        return parsed\n+\n+    decoder = json.JSONDecoder()\n+    for index, char in enumerate(text):\n+        if char != \"{\":\n+            continue\n+        try:\n+            parsed, end = decoder.raw_decode(text[index:])\n+        except json.JSONDecodeError:\n+            continue\n+        if isinstance(parsed, dict) and index + end <= len(text):\n+            return parsed\n+    return None\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

The implementation is going to mirror the real CLI: it will shell out per phase, read `.megaplan/plans/<name>/state.json` after each step, slice session logs using the pre-call message offset, and stop on either `ESCALATE` or the gate-iteration cap.

isinstance(session_id, str) and session_id:\n+                return session_id\n+    if history_entry:\n+        session_id = history_entry.get(\"session_id\")\n+        if isinstance(session_id,        key = f\"{agent}_planner\"\n+    elif step == \"critique\":\n+        key = f\"{agent}_critic\"\n+    elif step == \"gate\":\n+        key = f\"{agent}_gatekeeper\"\n+    elif step == \"finalize\":\n+        key = f\"{agent}_finalizer\"\n+    elif step == \"execute\":\n+        key = f\"{agent}_executor\"\n+    elif step == \"review\":\n+        key = f\"{agent}_reviewer\"\n+    else:\n+        key = f\"{agent}_{step}\"\n+    if model:\n+        import hashlib\n+\n+        key += f\"_{hashlib.sha256(model.encode()).hexdigest()[:8]}\"\n+    return key\n+\n+\n+def _latest_history_entry(state: dict[str, object], phase: str) -> dict[str, object]:\n+    history = state.get(\"history\", [])\n+    if not isinstance(history, list):\n+        return {}\n+    for entry in reversed(history):\n+        if isinstance(entry, dict) and entry.get(\"step\") == phase:\n+            return entry\n+    return {}\n+\n+\n+def _phase_iteration(state: dict[str, object], phase: 1)\n+\n+\n+def _read_phase_artifact(\n+    plan_dir: Path,\n+    state: dict[str, object],\n+    phase: str,\n+) -> tuple[str | None, object | None]:\n+    iteration = _phase_iteration(state, \"meta\": _read_json_file(meta_file),\n+            },\n+        )\n+    if phase == \"critique\":\n+        filename = f\"critique_v{iteration}.json\"\n+        return filename,  return (\n+            f\"revise_v{iteration}.json\",\n+            {\n+                \"plan_file\": plan_file.name,\n+                \"plan\": plan_file.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\") if phase == \"review\":\n+        return \"review.json\", _read_json_file(plan_dir / \"review.json\")\n+    return None, None\n+\n+\n+def _read_json_file(path: Path) -> object | None:\n+    if not path.exists():\n+        return None\n+    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n+\n+\n+def _as_str(value: object | None) -> str | None:\n+    return value if isinstance(value, str) > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

T13-T14 are sequential validation.\n\n**Biggest risk \u2014 T2 gate:** If EVAL.ts files overwhelmingly depend on Vercel-internal observability (Tier 3 fields), the scoring system becomes build-only and the eval results are much less meaningful. This is the earliest go/no-go decision point. Do T2 thoroughly before investing in T6 scoring implementation.\n\n**Four unresolved flags the gate passed through \u2014 executor must fix:**\n\n1. **scoring-git-diff-base-invalid:** The plan says `git diff HEAD~1` but there's only one commit in the workspace. FIX: capture initial_commit_sha from `git rev-parse HEAD` after the initial commit in prepare_workspace(), then diff against that SHA after execution. Or diff against the empty tree hash (`git diff 4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf899d15d4a9a96 HEAD`).\n\n2. **trace-capture-session-log-behavior-mismatched:** Plan says ~/.hermes/logs/ but actual path is ~/.hermes/sessions/. Also, sessions are REUSED per agent role across iterations (plan+revise share one session ID, all critiques share one, all gates share one). FIX: Before each megaplan CLI call, read the session log, record current message count as offset. After the call, re-read the log and slice messages[offset:] to get only that phase's messages. The session_id comes from state.json sessions object keyed by role name.\n\n3. **auditability-raw-api-capture-not-planned:** Accepted scope boundary (DECISION-004). Document in config schema and audit README that raw HTTP payloads are NOT captured \u2014 only normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(). This is sufficient for the experiment goals.\n\n4. **config-openrouter-params-not-plumbed:** Accepted scope boundary. openrouter_params in config are for documentation/reproducibility only. Add a clear comment in config.py and config JSON files that these values are NOT applied at runtime until Hermes/megaplan add inference parameter plumbing.\n\n**WorkerResult contract \u2014 get this right:** The actual fields are payload, raw_output, duration_ms, cost_usd, session_id, trace_output. Any code referencing output, input_tokens, or output_tokens is wrong. The worker function signature must be (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...) matching existing claude/codex workers.\n\n**Settled decisions (do not revisit):**\n- DECISION-001: Shell out to megaplan CLI, don't import as library\n- DECISION-002: File modification via git diff against initial commit, not tool-call mapping\n- DECISION-003: Traces from session log files, not SessionDB.get_messages_as_conversation()\n- DECISION-004: Cost from run_conversation() aggregates, not raw HTTP headers\n- DECISION-005: Phase machine is init\u2192plan\u2192critique\u2192gate\u2192(revise\u2192critique\u2192gate)*\u2192finalize\u2192execute\u2192review\n\n**No evals/ directory exists yet** \u2014 all files are net-new creation. > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

Also, sessions are REUSED per agent role across iterations (plan+revise share one session ID, all critiques share one, all gates share one). FIX: Before each megaplan CLI call, read the session log, record current message count as offset. After the call, re-read the log and slice messages[offset:] to get only that phase's messages. The session_id comes from state.json sessions object keyed by role name.\n\n3. **auditability-raw-api-capture-not-planned:** Accepted scope boundary (DECISION-004). > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent

        Idea:
Integrate the Vercel next-evals-oss evaluation suite into hermes-agent so it can be run via Hermes + megaplan with configurable models per phase, full auditability, and reproducibility.

The next-evals-oss repo (https://github.com/vercel/next-evals-oss) contains 21 Next.js coding evals. Each eval is a self-contained Next.js project with PROMPT.md (task for the agent) and EVAL.ts (vitest assertions, hidden from agent). The evals are synced from vercel/next.js canary branch via npm run sync-evals.

The goal is to run each eval through the full megaplan loop (plan, critique, gate, execute, review) using Hermes as the agent backend, with different models assigned to different phases. For the initial experiment: MiniMax M2.7 (minimax/minimax-m2.7 on OpenRouter) in Claude roles (plan, revise, gate, finalize) and GLM-5 (z-ai/glm-5 on OpenRouter) in Codex roles (critique, execute, review).

What needs to be built:

1. Orchestrator Script (evals/run_evals.py) - Python script that clones next-evals-oss, syncs evals, then for each eval: copies eval dir to clean workspace, runs npm install + next@canary, reads PROMPT.md as megaplan idea, shells out to megaplan CLI for full loop (init, plan, critique, gate with ITERATE handling max 3 iterations, finalize, execute --user-approved, review), scores result, collects artifacts. Config via JSON file specifying model routing per phase, max gate iterations, timeouts, which evals to run.

2. Scoring System - EVAL.ts files depend on __agent_eval__/results.json with observability data from Vercel framework. Generate compatible results.json from Hermes execution trace (tool calls, files modified, shell commands). Also check npm run build success as baseline score.

3. Full Audit Trail - For each eval run capture: all LLM API calls (full request/response), all tool executions (args + output), all megaplan artifacts (plans, critiques, gates, finalize, execution evidence), phase metadata (model, duration, tokens, cost), git diff of code changes, scoring results (build output, vitest output, verdict). Store in structured results/ directory.

4. Reproducibility - run-config.json captures git SHAs of all repos, model IDs, OpenRouter params, Node/npm versions, OS info. Auto-generate README.md with reproduction steps. Accept --config to replay experiments.

5. Hermes Trace Capture - Update hermes_worker.py to save full conversation history (result["messages"]) alongside WorkerResult. This contains all tool calls, responses, reasoning.

6. Implementation structure in hermes-agent repo:
evals/
  run_evals.py        - Main orchestrator  
  config.py           - Config schema and loading
  scoring.py          - Build checking, results.json generation, EVAL.ts running
  audit.py            - Trace capture, diff generation, README generation
  configs/
    minimax-glm5.json - MiniMax M2.7 + GLM-5 experiment config
    default.json      - Default config template

Key peculiarities: MiniMax M2.7 via OpenRouter uses chat_completions not native anthropic_messages; GLM-5 tool calling reliability for file/terminal ops is critical path; EVAL.ts observability format needs mapping from Hermes traces; each eval needs npm install next@canary setup; gate ITERATE loops need max iteration cap; cost tracking via OpenRouter response headers.

  "meta_commentary": "## Execution Guidance\n\n**Parallelism:** T1 (clone/catalog evals), T3 (model validation), and T0 (hermes-megaplan prerequisite) can all start immediately in parallel. T2 depends on T1. T4/T5/T6 depend on T2 (schema). T7-T9 are the core orchestrator and depend on T0 (hard blocker) + T4. T10 parallels T7-T9. T12 needs all implementation done. T13-T14 are sequential validation.\n\n**Biggest risk \u2014 T2 gate:** If EVAL.ts files overwhelmingly depend on Vercel-internal observability (Tier 3 fields), the scoring system becomes build-only and the eval results are much less meaningful. This is the earliest go/no-go decision point. Do T2 thoroughly before investing in T6 scoring implementation.\n\n**Four unresolved flags the gate passed through \u2014 executor must fix:**\n\n1. **scoring-git-diff-base-invalid:** The plan says `git diff HEAD~1` but there's only one commit in the workspace. FIX: capture initial_commit_sha from `git rev-parse HEAD` after the initial commit in prepare_workspace(), then diff against that SHA after execution. Or diff against the empty tree hash (`git diff 4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf899d15d4a9a96 HEAD`).\n\n2. **trace-capture-session-log-behavior-mismatched:** Plan says ~/.hermes/logs/ but actual path is ~/.hermes/sessions/. Also, sessions are REUSED per agent role across iterations (plan+revise share one session ID, all critiques share one, all gates share one). FIX: Before each megaplan CLI call, read the session log, record current message count as offset. After the call, re-read the log and slice messages[offset:] to get only that phase's messages. The session_id comes from state.json sessions object keyed by role name.\n\n3. **auditability-raw-api-capture-not-planned:** Accepted scope boundary (DECISION-004). Document in config schema and audit README that raw HTTP payloads are NOT captured \u2014 only normalized session logs and aggregate cost/token counters from run_conversation(). This is sufficient for the experiment goals.\n\n4. **config-openrouter-params-not-plumbed:** Accepted scope boundary. openrouter_params in config are for documentation/reproducibility only. Add a clear comment in config.py and config JSON files that these values are NOT applied at runtime until Hermes/megaplan add inference parameter plumbing.\n\n**WorkerResult contract \u2014 get this right:** The actual fields are payload, raw_output, duration_ms, cost_usd, session_id, trace_output. Any code referencing output, input_tokens, or output_tokens is wrong. The worker function signature must be (step, state, plan_dir, *, root, ...) matching existing claude/codex workers.\n\n**Settled decisions (do not revisit):**\n- DECISION-001: Shell out to megaplan CLI, don't import as library\n- DECISION-002: File modification via git diff against initial commit, not tool-call mapping\n- DECISION-003: Traces from session log files, not SessionDB.get_messages_as_conversation()\n- DECISION-004: Cost from run_conversation() aggregates, not raw HTTP headers\n- DECISION-005: Phase machine is init\u2192plan\u2192critique\u2192gate\u2192(revise\u2192critique\u2192gate)*\u2192finalize\u2192execute\u2192review\n\n**No evals/ directory exists yet** \u2014 all files are net-new creation. Structure: evals/{run_evals.py, config.py, scoring.py, audit.py, __init__.py}, evals/configs/{minimax-glm5.json, default.json}, evals/schemas/{results.schema.json}."
